# Uma Musume Reference
Uma Musume Reference Document

This document assumes you’ve completed and understood the tutorial. If you’re confused on how to actually play the game, refer to [this guide on Gametora](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://gametora.com/umamusume/beginners-guide&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375547407&usg=AOvVaw16ylqBzf-WeN0R80OYM223). They also have guides for each scenario under Articles > Scenario Guides. This is written for the Japanese server.

Older Versions: [URA Era](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://docs.google.com/document/d/1EApaW1p6TCJPBd_IHxAen2H9de70wI8cBqjEpes2ibo/edit%23&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375547868&usg=AOvVaw1QbyZ2TsF2wunqe1hxwyXh) | [Aoharu Era](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://docs.google.com/document/d/1gWl0peVbXsTjzvrgE8Qa7JtzbtoVRrNS5R_ZUHUcnj4/edit%23&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375548017&usg=AOvVaw1_f45CG4T_6ipwtKkFdfF3) | [MANT Era](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://docs.google.com/document/d/1FsehH8LjMvs764qvvpCn7LjbO7R_uBpxfzllhtdsQU4/edit%23&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375548147&usg=AOvVaw2QfqxEBFakiFQrrQY-8GQV) | [GL Era](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://docs.google.com/document/d/1R6N7w8_M_E-yIwkwGzWqQ9Fg71OoZh3gCIdWUPcvfH0/edit?usp%3Dsharing&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375548303&usg=AOvVaw3jOsmq6LDQFWNI93Q9hj2G)

The formatting is a bit weird but it should perform better.

This is basically the total sum of my knowledge of the game gathered from months of asking and answering questions on the Discord. It should be able to answer most questions you have about the game and its mechanics. When I learn something new, I’ll add it here. Reading it from front to back isn’t recommended, simply use the table of contents to find the section related to your questions. Though, I’ve tried to order it in a logical way.

"Should I pull on the current banner?” (Almond Eye / Forever Young Int, Casino Drive Friend)

Uma:

If you like her.

Almond Eye is a semi-limited Uma, which means there is a 4.5% 3\* chance (but the rate-up is 0.75% as usual). She is a Turf-locked Leader with separate evolutions for Mile and Medium distance races. Medium distance is stronger, but she's still very good for Mile. Her ult gives both early midleg speed (stronger in Medium) and last spurt speed. One of her awakenings gives later half midleg speed, while the other awakening depending on distance either gives more later half midleg speed and perfect accel, or random speed in the second half of the race.

This is a very generic kit with very high numbers, so you can expect her to be a mainstay in Mile and Medium races. That being said, we only have one Medium turf PvP event announced (March), and she has no ult evolution, so you do not have to pull if you do not like her.

Cards:

Yes, obviously you should pull. It's anniversary.

Forever Young is an Int card with two sets of two gold skills, one Dirt-focused and one weaker, but extremely generic one. She powercreeps every single Int card that came before. She also has scenario link. If you MLB this card, you will likely not need another int card for the next six months or more. There's not much else to say; get this card MLB or suffer for a while.

Casino Drive is a Friend card, and therefore necessary for this scenario. We do not yet know whether we need her at a certain LB or not.

You get 120 free rolls on the support card banner. Ideally, you wait until these rolls are over to invest the rest of your rolls, but 12 days is a long time - considering the scenario will be around 120 days long, it's 10% of the scenario that you are not playing. Because of that, you can think about investing 80 or 280 of your own rolls right away to go up to 200 or 400 with free rolls. Just be aware that sometimes, this will backfire on you.

Event Schedule

February 14 - March 31: Various anniversary buffs (bonus rentals, bonus inheritance rerolls, anniversary shop + missions)

February 24: 5th Anniversary

Late March: Champions Meeting Classic (2200m Del Mar Turf)

Late April: Champions Meeting Dirt (2000m Del Mar Dirt)

Late May: League of Heroes (1200m Kyoto Turf)

Coming from Global
------------------

If you want to play on the JP server after having tried Global, there’s a few differences to be aware of. Okay, a lot of differences. Note that the JP and Global servers both try to save their data to the same place, so playing both on PC is unexpectedly tricky without using an emulator.

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztbFURm5g9lO0Bn3WR1mm1Waln--wuXFchie5pPZQb1YUkEPmc8hRt5D7SmvsX9EF8B0c_ssVFVfjLtp9cyXXKY5p_fyie48ujs2nwFoC4_vaIYXpZhSv9blydPW-ei0d_EQY2S1FOsY2vMyUcJW=s600)

First, you should wait for a free pulls event to reroll on. They happen at least once every 4 months, at the start of each scenario and on the half- and full anniversaries. If you’re reading this right now, there will be one in about a week for half-anni. That will let you start with an up-to-date MLB SSR or two. Setting your Stadium team is probably going to get you a billion jewels as a new player with current umas.

Then, save your jewels for the next scenario banner for another good SSR or two, pull some umas you think are cute, and you’ll be on your way to competitiveness.

When doing runs for competitive umas, you should play Design Your Island. Normally on JP, the scenarios are distance-specific (eg you train short/mile in one, mid/long in another, etc) but they really messed up with DYI and it’s just the best for everything right now. Feel free to check out the others for fun, though. There are guides for most of them here.

Design Your Island is quite easy to get into, coming from Global. When the bar on the left fills halfway, you get a ticket, and you can use that ticket to trigger all your trainings at once basically. When it fills fully, you get a new ticket, but NOT if you still have one, so you have to use the ticket before the bar fills. Otherwise the training is quite similar to URA. Bond your cards, do the rainbows. For setting up the island facilities, refer to [Celery’s guide](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://docs.google.com/document/d/1kmCbUtdQap3YtXnnGRrdk-_Di3pXaATnJBrPACPQcAc/edit?usp%3Dsharing&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375589061&usg=AOvVaw23bWAQ0H4S7l1e9J0mMI4v) and just copy the recommendation.

Tucker provides recreations you can go on with her, just like Tazuna and Aoi do on Global. Each time you do, your supports get a specialty rate bonus on the next turn, increasing their chance of rainbowing. You can turn down the points in the resulting event to try for better ticket usage.

You can also use the rental decks, which should be in your list somewhere. For the most part, you should stick to the first deck, or the last one when training Long since it gives recoveries. These decks are not that good, but when you have no cards, they’ll be quite serviceable. In DYI, borrow [Tucker](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://gametora.com/umamusume/supports/30257-tucker-bryne&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375590523&usg=AOvVaw2NgIqea1jShMYREraS5gwC) as the sixth card every time. On JP, each scenario has a mandatory friend card, and Tucker is that for DYI.

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztZ6nw7cE1Etbz656mxxjXp-F15o_WatRa4wKrFFe5mwD5Vku9CMgJxqAbANY1LOKtYPMnFAPhQQZmulMbENJTj39XLOx6lXNwMk9AZiZzy5gmgEhxl6lOlW5iN9rsAytjlI-iOGS5xhPXzD1g=s600)

There’s also an extra fast skip option in the menu to make runs go faster. You’ll probably want to set that.

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztakyKocxh6M0kXME7y0Zl3rwdqniazmrc7z-Ls1tGbnoDyidkVnHfp0UBzsdm5eq5BgbLGWPbZpAQshhemTD1LjZxUUpf6MKlHZv4bpdsV7TuS4JnmLNA6MZThhgiiW_mFCxb1WawIGHfpgkqHl=s600)

It’s worth noting that the game now has an in-game event viewer. Click the little button at the bottom right of an event with choices and a popup will show you what each choice gives. No need to have Gametora open in another tab. See the [Common Terms](#h.q52kkhbbrqui) section to identify what they are. Learning Katakana would be a big help in general.

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztblg9Otnq21OqCyhfwU_QSIFNO2XgmPmw3MGCdBsVw8ZjXT47XU77EadegTbufjyCEVS7LiiX14h75hc-sQBSkLyH3yBHnf7eEyJn_wArAwTEUhxt9qnvCPgG7r231WBAVwGN83wUudq3vYJQ=s600)

You can also play runs automatically, using the orange button labelled “おまかせ” (“I’ll leave it to you”) near the top right.

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztakYPSxtCEqo3XsmFaeO_-iPRFNMuqM6Fs4eIeCf9EZE_RBh7vgOyP0jjRUPZ8X1iXsXnbpdoTAp5eHOchR8HP2AXFORO3wqf6HhcfHZKCP1DrIBXnefrH6EjSmSuXgXdT53iP8T5OK37DMpeCf=s600)

You’ll want to set your phone’s screen to not turn off, as it plays through them in real time. Choose one of the custom settings and set all these values to low:

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztbjNUb13vBnp5fhcJKav6SvoqLYKskeYZV5YEzkvbf0iMup7N61_xI4Tnz_8vIo0M5ji1IOGd2kczn6TT0d6-U1E9IjzdgW0IhIkypiqCpNioi9A0w9S8TaVWCK2lOmyMhqfR_yd9Tae9OQYQ=s600)

If you don’t do that, the auto won’t use the tickets. Then, you can set the facility order below that:

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztbkuf93MGQjHQmfwcsSiT2VJbyHOFcZTNlK8mnD6QjRb88OjZAhy9qXx4QIiOaZpP-KdPlJnTFGzSfso02MhcEvPMGO6FQAeI-7Kj5xtkZBzQFa6OVwL-zSo2aFctjyUnArliqGxakurb0aCuYP=s600)

Finally, check all these boxes near the bottom to tell auto to do races when you need fans and use clocks when you fail:

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztY2T6_GnkaA4h3LTnGSXilmaL_5Hj3ch3vw4HsFu5rDFEDvE2CToAcR06URfUwNJJdqyxdH0K7HVGrVBfaOyElkz5PlfbnjQSttt4rXYGe2tBWn02nN8zH36eYZcgl1KrdJXNZqq2Whz3l46nao=s600)

These umas won’t be competitive, but it’s very useful for making parents or getting event points.

Speaking of making parents, you can reroll your sparks at the end of the run. You can choose which one to keep after rerolling, so there’s no downside (outside of the TP cost). I wouldn’t recommend spending gems doing it.

Speaking of event points, you may have noticed a new button on the home page.

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztaf49-tRMFxIkjtg47aq-PjS6i4mCpMRD1z75OCzyrtHb8rcblyTvRuyPdLYZCsiaO6JKSHr1apF5XeCWHe96Aixp2vIx4aacYzWo2nC4-I_20dryDmU5q9w4BKPg23_tnAproCuC2GPsN3Zw=s600)

The suitcase will let you do Expeditions, which generate event points. Even if you don’t do any runs, you can get most of the event done solely through this. There’s another important new button over where you do the daily races:

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztZJ39KVtu_qj3NpOmV1MzecA3r5YLgTd5C-kmwzY2ScUX3ZeavLVtkYVtP2l0lFbGoDw_WfSX-dvLgSnzljzuaVJVfoaYGSflBc0xzh8rXFXtIjpoMazVAsZNvja6gsZMKDCRG-XfLnmOzBSRGM=s600)

The button on the right is Daily Legend Races. Each one you complete will give you 250 jewels. You can also repeatedly do one to get pieces for the uma you race against, letting you eventually unlock the uma for free. The best targets from a meta perspective are Valentine’s Bourbon, Taiki Shuttle, and Copano Rickey.

Now, over to the story side of things. Find the button that says エクストラ (“Extra”), then within that, ストーリーエベント (“Story Event”). Here’s where you can see the past stories, unlocking them with the green bookmark item. You can also get the welfare SSRs from here. There are three notable welfares, Speed Fine Motion, Stamina Biwa, and Int Helios. Also you’re gonna have to start calling Wit Int. They can fill some gaps while you’re getting gacha SSRs and upgrading from the rental deck. They’re from these stories:

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztYfYEvwQSj0WBTjGvXRkyZqWgq3F-9V535h5nmGdnTACGHew0sFQBkup-Fg_yk1uPWrRCEFLCp3WT5mMmXIkgSDueKH_teqDkf0_YRWhKK64ITqZyyiSJdk3OfapNxu6o3TpOOPgPr5psOWmw=s600)

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztbDtjsvod1AaRELP4JCfWAzEcW_sROTbNDAhnzQopDn8Fe8MQiWEFL0309DQGMK65-k2-71VaVR7kNhAmIxkpHRyqp53lUEF0Z9iBwOeU9Yg4Y5t2CdTj46UgjdU-y8osU6fMHONPvPLD41ymUX=s600)

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztYQ-6aMYinqii8SRtneGqpArvnszVkNnPV1dOvn8uxWBSPvZFYyGtR3HNBHfETfKybgx0sa12Lao23qAbfX_EV2vynGfPS4N1HiN23ldt6NtCX3ZAgF4sA1G2RPgZW5qfwDADaQN3u-iFjwieqk=s600)

You should also, of course, grab all the jewels from the main story and stuff while you’re here. You get two free umas from doing it, Special Week and Rhein Kraft. Kraft is a great parent on certain tracks, while Spe is reasonable in Arima.

Finally, in the shop, there’s a blue ticket store. You get these from the monthly battle pass thing that got added. You can exchange them for old cards, which will also help you fill in gaps in your deck.

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztan0IRca4Dyp1TdnhibNqU3fp6Xw1voDoLBuD8ESMNYiIKOiqiP2j8df-S1S2iHdp4afb9cNnTHdpwGBtkUhOhJAWxenpGyND4hQohnYKLjY6eGj230hzohNRqiKmK8lUk8ACEp-zm5mvcVVw=s600)

The cards you should buy will depend on what cards you rerolled for, but in general aim for these:

Speed - Jungle Pocket, Maruzensky

Stamina - Super Creek (not filler, she’s still a top tier card)

Int - TM Opera O

Urara from the friend points shop can fill your Guts needs better than any of the cards in this shop, and none of the Power cards are good.

Rerolling Guide
---------------

Rerolling in Uma Musume is extremely fast, as you don’t have to redownload the game and can skip the tutorial after the first time, so it’s highly recommended. However, you cannot reroll on DMM, you’ll have to do it on a phone or on an emulator.

If you get a reroll you like, you can start playing with it. If you get a good account but want to keep rerolling to try to get something better, you can set a [Link Password](#h.bnybq3a3f2l1) for the account so you can go back to it. To judge whether a reroll is good, refer to the [Reroll Tier List](#h.xozyxwyqwog2).

If you get a bad reroll, you can delete the account data from the main menu. Doing it this way means you don’t have to re-download the game.

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztbQv9jm047zcQLjoFrF-J7JdjJeJkOk75waslfFBw5NXZeK313KdEVvSU0ubCrUhaMmyUY09gRLBGBIE-Z5djrll3LqYW5uuqS-DvC7Sa2Bya970vzqp8xhE5L7Knx6tzZmvto7WVIeYc6NSLB_S5ZaCs6TiB7n2pA=s600)

On future rerolls, make sure to press the green button on this popup, as that’s what skips the tutorial. It doesn’t appear on the first account you make on a device.

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztY7-9q0H-anJoid5JFUM8vON85JwNxYcJWs_9pcZwjaXQmZeldp_3pO-XegyptbtBcVbH5fOv9PWLmo1x4ksifdKu9yly3wY5rhJu1G4Bvs6smryFXY6H4mtNYLBG7p4Z0VEdYCOp722vahXNEZxi0ATfQuZENi6Mz6vA=s600)

Using BlueStacks’ multi-instancing, you can run many copies of the game at the same time with your inputs synced across them, which will make rerolling drastically quicker. If you have a decent PC, it’s certainly worth it over using a phone.

When rerolling, you should spend everything on Support Cards. They are what determine the power of your account. Even the most meta umas can’t perform well in PvP with Rare cards. If the banner uma is someone you really like, you can try to get both it and a good starting card.

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztbkxaRMhFvSOfJ136a_FbhOKWNFr_G028fBz-tZ0yk3mdrZgCEZAWAmqVx_K65NbxHP6lAXK0dQdgTxh011rBm-q1N8C1r5pqTvx0Y4_PTjC46Cx6eGICP2qqqhWq_7Am3i_-9lELS-pP6aU5ZVLs7g9ptZU0fYmnAExQ=s600)

There will also be some banners that take tickets, depending on what promotions are going on when you make the account. They’ll only be shown if you own the tickets. You can freely spend these tickets. The ticket banners never update so there is no reason to save them.

If you have an almost-good account, there are a few things you can quickly do to get an extra roll. If you create a [Link Password](#h.bnybq3a3f2l1), you’ll get 1000 jewels. And if you check your [Uma Directory](#h.xqe6w96boc31), you’ll get some jewels. Accepting the rewards from the missions (which will be things like “collect 45 different support cards”) can also move the needle.

If you get really desperate, you can set your birthday in your [profile](#h.cj3ef3rtuve6) and change your title, or start reading the [uma stories](#h.wnsy64egwuzg). You could also break down some unneeded cards for [horseshoes](#h.y1i9km39q95m) to buy tickets, depending on how many duplicates you pulled.

Rerolling Tier List
-------------------

Here��s a general reroll tier list for players who don’t plan on spending much, or any, money on the game. When it comes to SSRs, it’s quality over quantity. Between an account with one top tier SSR and one with 20 random ones, the former is better.

If you’re on one of the other servers, then check the Banner History at the bottom of [this page](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://gametora.com/umamusume/gacha&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375604043&usg=AOvVaw0J_dWPRDOyF2xlZTPF7xZg) and find where you currently are. Then, look ahead for good banners.

Best Cards to Reroll For:

Most SSRs are weaker than SRs until high LBs, but there are some exceptions. Those exceptions are what you want to target. There’s also a pick ticket in the missions that can get you another copy, which you should use for these. Reroll for one copy, then ticket the other.

Wisdom McQueen: [McQueen](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://gametora.com/umamusume/supports/30172-mejiro-mcqueen&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375605079&usg=AOvVaw29gP9vfC9I3ARS7k9KhNPs) is usable at low limit breaks, beats a lot of the MLB SR wisdom cards even at 0LB, and is currently the best in slot for stats. Leaders are generally good for most events and her skill and hint pool are leader focused.

Maruzensky: [Maruzensky](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://gametora.com/umamusume/supports/30107-maruzensky&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375605732&usg=AOvVaw0Hlc_1e6VuXCF8pFNllQMh) is a competitive Speed card at 1LB with impactful Runner skills. She’s particularly good for Runners (McQueen, Twin Turbo, Seiun Sky, Kitasan).

Sounds of Earth: [Sounds of Earth](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://gametora.com/umamusume/supports/30173-sounds-of-earth&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375606411&usg=AOvVaw2z7AV5PBHYrZ6qpqnkOluO) gives a gold recovery that works everywhere and is also pretty strong. Gold recoveries can be worth as much as 250+ Stamina, which really helps for Mid and Long distance trainings. You only need one copy of either of these, so you can save the pick ticket for something else. Though, if you don’t have them, the [McQueen](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://gametora.com/umamusume/supports/30022-mejiro-mcqueen&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375607168&usg=AOvVaw0F--UaiOxTkNxYnygpRV1D) welfare works fine for Long, but her gold isn’t guaranteed.

There isn’t a good card that’s particularly good for Betweeners. Also note that, on a new account, it’s fairly easy to raise the currency to do 200 total rolls on a banner, as long as you didn’t start the account very close to the banner’s end. Upon doing 200 rolls, you can pick one of the banner cards. You can use this combined with patience to get a reroll that quickly gets an MLB card.

You can see the [Support Card Tier Lists](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://euophrys.github.io/uma-tiers/&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375608556&usg=AOvVaw3ozF0L_zlVOlJm4GgYf7Bh) for more details. While there are very strong SSRs in the game, it’s nearly impossible to reroll for them if they’re not on rate up. For example, even though Kitasan Black is widely regarded as one of the best cards in the game, that’s only at MLB. Before 3LB, she’s quite average or even bad, so rerolling for her isn’t a good idea.

Link Passwords
--------------

Link Passwords allow you to connect your account to other devices.

Creating a Link Password for your account is pretty important, as it will prevent you from losing your account if anything bad happens. If you somehow lose your game data, you can use the Link Password to get it back. Even if you have the account linked with something else, like Google Play, you should still make a Link Password just in case. It also nets you 1000 Jewels.

Creating a Link Password
------------------------

Open the menu in the top right to get started, then click these buttons.

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztbxtqPK4282A4lmM8eOj_w_pYj00qVl_ZuLn1iiOl1KWFmXkL_8OZXXU1f41cgJoWvahYLLDJXAcVd7pPf-XmzappckaYA-HtDLC5IEuk2VGSFif_3VS8xCisoOQW6YdKcTcKA3E2LxZJ7VY-ht-XTshMckQm-ZVckolQ=s600)

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztYLaPG5g5XlUrlp5SE2r9yeekX8RUJILaAbBka8rI1GDbF84nMEBR8BBN8OtD2z37eujGRqVolzpXNilHprOtbP4AyLERVOjwarZy_TRLHcXgMco3BHlgBWTuZC36_y5Y63TICUNdp6W60CiDF0jF9-yzdmr5CB1Sy8ig=s600)

The password must contain a capital letter, a number, and be at least 8 characters long.

Write down the ID and Password it gives you in the sixth screen and keep it somewhere safe, preferably not on the same device you play Uma Musume on. Otherwise, if you lose the device or it somehow stops working, you’d lose both.

Using a Link Password
---------------------

Transferring your account is a similar process. Open the game on the device you want to transfer to and have the ID and Password from before handy. You can access the Link Password screen from either the title screen or the ingame menu.

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztYs_sj70YJXaDlAKiZFkf-QiibHeAPt9Kg7oQBmOuD8i_n3rhqcnP-itdoqwH2OEeDLXU3TrNc_E2kMguGv6LGwvAjTb6FqZqfutVc2hqDigLZfTRDdG3PHTFSizOYwQ49PuiZkzP-1ek56jtNb1OkCoSZAWTp3tCv3ag=s600)

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztYx-l8FFES2H_74gSoJMEQk4rLHFonbhm-mXBjjaqynT2nkS0PyF507jDw7PN60rvNRKi9lIDsIrBPcXdgw44O5FB-RPYjmX7KiMIiFmHslCf8JvwkzKxfDDl-4qH6z0hsb8dMV3CgVeX58cbh03CxdES3r184qQvc=s600)

Doing this will not remove the account from the old device. You can have your account on both your phone and on DMM, for example. You just can’t play them both at the same time, logging in to one will log you out of the other.

You can use the same Link Password multiple times.

The data on the device that gets overwritten will be lost forever if you didn’t make a Link Password for it.

Your First Month In Uma
-----------------------

There’s a lot to do when you first start an account. Here’s a checklist, roughly in order of when you should do them.

*   [Reroll](#h.mv6tx2to7362) if you’re going to. It’s extremely helpful.
*   Level up cards that you take on runs, but not others. You have a limited amount of resources for levelling things up in the beginning and it’s better to take a small amount of cards to max than level a bunch a little bit. Check the [builds here](#h.l684u5ruz1nq).
*   Read the [Basic Training Strategy](#h.c4ekqevsx9rd). It applies in all scenarios.
*   [Add some friends](#h.fqwqsx3btwai) with good parents and rent them while doing runs.
*   Win every graded race once. When going to the [optional race](#h.nsxowqeb4qtk) screen, if there’s a grayed-out trophy in the top right, you haven’t won that race. Each one you win for the first time gives you jewels, so try to clear each once.
*   If any uma you train results in a three star blue, or two star blue and three star pink, click the carrot icon in the top left and mark them as a good parent you shouldn’t delete. Use umas like this as parents when starting runs once you get them. [Inheritance details here](#h.4qbf55ysbjo9).
*   Spark on the current support card banner if you rerolled on it (200 total pulls) to get at least one more copy of the banner card.
*   Do 30-50 rolls on umas to collect the 1 and 2 star umas. After this, start saving up jewels for either umas you like, or better support cards. Ideally, you shouldn’t pull without a spark ready (30,000 jewels). Two sparks gives you a [50/50 chance](#h.4h978qax10ir) of MLBing an SSR.
*   Train an uma for each [Stadium](#h.ffg71a6svcim) distance. For brand new players, that would be Bakushin in Short, Vodka in Mile, Daiwa in Mid, Gold Ship in Long, and Urara in Dirt.
*   With those umas, you should be able to clear the [main story](#h.wnsy64egwuzg) to get the free SSRs. You can do a run with the [Team Sirius](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://gametora.com/umamusume/supports/30081-team-sirius&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375617231&usg=AOvVaw0mW4oxZYdnfjpZ4KxLo25G) card and buy the gold skill, then use that uma in the final Arima race to unlock the free [Special Week](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://gametora.com/umamusume/characters/100103-special-week&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375617582&usg=AOvVaw3beLZ59bX_kQx9r-oViLme), a Long Distance uma.
*   You can also start doing each of the [Daily Legend Races](#h.ppqqbn74fy3f). Each one you clear will give you jewels, and with an uma for each distance, you should be able to clear them all. Once all are finished, you can start farming the uma you like best.
*   Join a [Circle](#h.q5k0974asyw) with many members and start trading shoes for [Trainer Medals](#h.peph7846fppq), which can be used to limit break the free SSRs from the story. You can additionally get up to 100 medals per day from people borrowing your uma. You can use reroll accounts to do this yourself each day, if you want to speed things up.
*   Finish off any of the [missions](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://gametora.com/umamusume/missions/permanent&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375618888&usg=AOvVaw3X_-4ss_jmJqUYbrI0xrmq) you’re still missing. Once they’re complete, you can use the random gacha tickets, then use the 3\* Uma select ticket with less fear of getting duplicates later.
*   Each week, you’ll go up a Stadium class and unlock another 5 slots, so keep filling them in as that happens. You’ll get a lot of jewels and friendship points from the class upgrades and team rank upgrades. Here’s a [full F2P team](#h.rbnaasa6ngsy).
*   Use the Friendship Points gained from Stadium to get all the copies of Urara from the Friendship Point shop. Once you have her MLB, go for the follow slots.
*   Prepare some umas for the [Champion’s Meeting](#h.n319dua1dy0z) or League of Heroes for the month. Even if you lose, you’ll still get rewards, so it doesn’t hurt to participate.

Tips for New Players
--------------------

1.  Save your 3\* uma pick ticket until you finish the [missions](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://gametora.com/umamusume/missions/permanent&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375620493&usg=AOvVaw3zmN5koWQTJ5Bj2xZ7OI0s). The missions grant you tickets that give a random 3\* uma, so you want to avoid getting dupes in this way.

*   If you have a favourite uma among those nine, you can use the ticket on them if the random tickets didn’t give you her (you can get Special Week for free later in the story though). Otherwise, use it on an uma with Dirt aptitude, such as Oguri Cap or Maruzensky. These are the rarest, and there are only two umas below 3\* that have it naturally. Oguri Cap is the strongest uma in the starting set.

2.  Join a [Circle](#h.ue7l4f9cq561) as soon as you can, preferably one with many members. These are like guilds. By giving people the shoes they request, you will receive [Trainer Medals](#h.peph7846fppq), which can be used to limit break the story SSR cards.

3.  Don’t buy anything from the random shops that sometimes appear, except for clocks. Money is very precious in this game and the items in the shops are not very valuable. The pennants can be worth it if you absolutely know you need that certain pennant.

*   If you collect 150 pieces for a 3\* uma, you can exchange them to get that uma. This is quite expensive, costing over 2 million money per uma, and would take years. But, you can buy ones that match [daily legend race](#h.ppqqbn74fy3f) umas to save a day. If you don’t pull on umas, getting the 1\* and 2\* shards to unlock their outfit is fine.

4.  [Borrow other players’ umas](#h.bio8lk9a88e1) as parents whenever you can. You can do this five times a day. At the start, your parents will give +15 stats, while a borrowed parent can give +63 stats. There are also the two inheritance events, so your own give roughly 45 total, while the rented ones give roughly 189 total. Additionally, any uma you make with the rented +63 parents will give at least +31 stats (~+91 total), making your start much better.

5.  SR cards are strong. They’re easy to get duplicates of, so typically you’ll be using limit broken SRs rather than 0LB SSRs (unless you want the gold skills from those SSRs). Some MLB SRs are even stronger than MLB SSRs. My suggestion is generally to pull on support cards until you get usable MLB SRs, then save your jewels for umas you like or to do a large amount of rolls on good SSRs ([on average](#h.4h978qax10ir) it takes 400 rolls to MLB an SSR card).

6.  Don’t use auto for anything. Whether it’s [picking decks](#h.l684u5ruz1nq), picking parents, or picking your [PvP team](#h.ffg71a6svcim), the game’s pretty bad at it. Learn how to do them yourself as soon as you can.

7.  Make a [Link Password](#h.bnybq3a3f2l1). If anything goes wrong, it will prevent you from losing your account, and you get some gems for doing so anyway.

Common Terms
------------



* Japanese: スピード
  * English: Speed
* Japanese: スタミナ
  * English: Stamina
* Japanese: パワー
  * English: Power
* Japanese: 根性
  * English: Guts
* Japanese: 賢さ
  * English: Wisdom, Intelligence, Wit
* Japanese: 体力
  * English: Energy
* Japanese: やる気
  * English: Motivation
* Japanese: 芝
  * English: Turf
* Japanese: ダート
  * English: Dirt
* Japanese: 短距離
  * English: Short (Distance), Sprint. Distances from 1000m to 1400m
* Japanese: マイル
  * English: Mile, Distances from 1500m to 1800m.
* Japanese: 中距離
  * English: Mid (Distance), Medium (Distance). 1900m to 2400m
* Japanese: 長距離
  * English: Long (Distance). 2500m to 3600m
* Japanese: 大逃げ
  * English: Oonige, Great Escape, True Runner
* Japanese: 逃げ
  * English: Runner, Escape, Nige, Front
* Japanese: 先行
  * English: Leader, Senkou, Pace
* Japanese: 差し
  * English: Betweener, Sashi, Late
* Japanese: 追込
  * English: Chaser, Oikomi, End
* Japanese: 因子
  * English: Factors (inheritance), Sparks
* Japanese: Other Terms
  * English: Definition
* Japanese: Rainbow
  * English: Also known as friendship training. The rainbow glow around trainings.
* Japanese: Bond
  * English: The gauges in the top right when selecting a training. Visual here
* Japanese: Spark
  * English: Aka pity. Choosing an SSR/3* after doing 200 rolls on the same banner.
* Japanese: Welfare
  * English: A card given for free, such as event cards and the ones from the story.
* Japanese: Hint
  * English: The !s that appear on trainings and grant skills.
* Japanese: Shorthand
  * English: Definition
* Japanese: Agemasen
  * English: When an SSR card can fail to give you their gold skill, or fails to do so.
* Japanese: CM
  * English: Champion’s Meeting, the monthly PvP tournament
* Japanese: Futa
  * English: Cover 蓋, a Runner who tries to prevent other Runners from doing things
* Japanese: Ult
  * English: An uma’s built-in unique skill (from “ultimate”)
* Japanese: LB
  * English: Limit Break. Usually preceded by a number, like 2LB or 0LB.
* Japanese: MLB
  * English: Max Limit Break. The same as 4LB, for now.
* Japanese: Skills
  * English: Full Name
* Japanese: A lot of the time, people will use the Japanese names because they’re shorter or they don’t know the English names. Here are some further shortenings.
  * English: 
* Japanese: Jiga
  * English: Jigatame 地固め, the Japanese name for Groundwork.
* Japanese: Gear
  * English: Maruzenksy’s unique skill, 紅焔ギア/LP1211-M
* Japanese: Agatte
  * English: Agattekita アガッてきた！, the Japanese name for Now I’m Pumped!
* Japanese: Jaja
  * English: Jajauma じゃじゃウマ娘, the Japanese name for Wild Girl
* Japanese: Chokki
  * English: Chokusen ikki 直線一気, the Japanese name for Straight Shot
* Japanese: Uma Names
  * English: Full Name
* Japanese: Many Uma shorthands are obvious, but here are some of the more confusing ones.
  * English: 
* Japanese: Unsu
  * English: Seiun Sky (セイウンスカイ Sei - un su - kai)
* Japanese: Ayabe
  * English: Admire Vega (アドマイヤベガ A - domai - ya be - ga)
* Japanese: MTG
  * English: Mayano Top Gun, not Magic The Gathering
* Japanese: NTR
  * English: Narita Top Road. It’s Narita Top Road.
* Japanese: Kaichou
  * English: Symboli Rudolf, the student council president
* Japanese: Chocobon
  * English: Valentine’s Mihono Bourbon, from Chocolate + Bourbon and an IRL snack
* Japanese: Neicha
  * English: Nice Nature, from her Japanese name
* Japanese: Mun
  * English: Matikanetannhauser. She makes “mun” sounds.


Frequently Asked Questions
--------------------------

Why can’t I put this uma I just pulled on my PvP team?

You didn’t train them yet. Read [Gametora’s Beginner Guide](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://gametora.com/umamusume/beginners-guide&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375647626&usg=AOvVaw2VISTZo1V-ZxV3xu0GKHNS).

Is this banner worth pulling on?

See the answers on the first page of this document.

Is this card I just pulled good?

0LB SSRs will be worse than SRs with LBs, so probably not. Check the [Tier List](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://euophrys.github.io/uma-tiers/&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375648325&usg=AOvVaw24TTr_oDJCXP17RJyEvdsc).

What build should I use for X?

CM or Stadium? Stadium/General \-> [click here](#h.l684u5ruz1nq) CM -> [click here](#h.qrhuggz0naks)

What parent should I use for X / what unique should I inherit for X?

CM or Stadium?

Stadium \-> [click here](#h.111mp5atpat9)

CM -> [click here](#h.qrhuggz0naks)

What Scenario should I play in?

The newest scenario will be the best, whichever is furthest right. Currently, that’s L’Arc. Others can be used for making parents, but aren’t currently used competitively.

Who should I use for loop breeding for X?

Don’t do loop breeding unless you’re prepared to do 500 runs. You can safely forget it exists, it gives little benefit over normal training. Just see the [Beginning Breeding Strategy](#h.mol9c2gd15jp).

Why did I lose this race even though all my stats are way higher than the opponents’?

You probably got blocked. Your uma can get surrounded by other umas and be unable to move sometimes. It just happens, nothing you can really do about it.

How do I win this race? (eg for a mission, or in the main story, or anything)

The most common issue is lack of Stamina. Refer to the [Required Stamina Chart](#h.5gejlihfmr29). Lower it by 600 in training. If your Stamina is fine, probably your Power is the issue. Make sure your uma also has A for the [surface and distance](#h.czg4cxp5ww7j).

What skills should I buy here?

Pick one distance and one strategy your uma has A or better in. Find their Japanese form in the [Common Terms](#h.q52kkhbbrqui) chart. Buy skills that have that Japanese at the end of the skill. For example, if the skill ends in ＜作戦・先行＞ it’s for Leaders, and if it ends with ＜長距離＞, it’s for Long. You can also buy skills that have nothing in angle brackets at the end, unless they’re at the top of the list (uniques). That’s fine to start. For more advanced buying criteria, see the [Skill Rankings](#h.4tonsji7onb5).

Do debuffs affect my own umas in team races?

That’d make them pretty pointless. No, they don’t.

Do debuffs stack?

Yes. All skills stack. Yes, even if they have the same name.

Can I check how many races I’ve done while still in training?

Open the menu bottom right and click the button with the trophy icon.

Does this skill work outside of training?

Yes. Any skill you can buy can be used anywhere.

When will X uma be released?

Find the horse on [JBIS](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://www.jbis.jp/horse/&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375653679&usg=AOvVaw2_kI2dniL9sBvRvEyjYWKl) and look at the bottom of their page for what races they won. They will usually be released around one of those. For example, [Nishino Flower](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://www.jbis.jp/horse/0000224549/&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375653965&usg=AOvVaw3mpXkcWWKtc1yZ34lqESP6) was released on April 11th, and she won the Oka Sho on April 12th. They don’t always follow this, but it’s the only method you can use to guess.

Can green skills increase your stats beyond the cap?

Yes.

How do I get rid of umas I don’t want?

Go to the [Hall of Fame](#h.pe87ab7i1ogh) and press the button in the bottom right, then click the umas you want to sell. Double check their [inheritance](#h.4qbf55ysbjo9) first, you wouldn’t want to accidentally sell a good parent.

Do we know what the next banner is?

Unless the banner is announced on a Pakalive stream, they will post the banner details exactly twenty-four hours before the banner changes on their [Twitter](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://twitter.com/uma_musu/&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375655363&usg=AOvVaw3Ftk43dzSj_Tb9hj8FFicV). Accurate leaks are very rare.

When will X get rerun?

Umas very rarely get rerun. There’s usually one banner of support card reruns per month, but there’s no pattern to the cards that get rerun. In short, we have no idea.

Is this character limited?

No, there are no limited umas. Other than event welfares, there are also no limited support cards. Events haven’t been rerun yet, but surely will at some point.

When does this banner/event end?

The end times listed in-game are in your local time.

Which card do I spend my Trainer Medals on?

This is answered in the [Trainer Medal](#h.peph7846fppq) section.

Why don’t we see Erzzy on the EN Discord anymore?

The short answer is I don’t like the staff.

Originally, the members of #character-building ended up forming close friendships. Uma only requires meta discussion for about the week when a CM is announced and the days the CM is actually running, so in the other times we mostly talked about whatever and got to know each other.

Then one day, staff told us that there would be no more off-topic discussions allowed. So, we all moved to a different server (with difficulty, as they prevented us from organising such a thing on the Discord itself). We got to continue our off-topic talks with even more freedom, staff got a channel that turned so inactive it got merged into #gameplay-questions, and everyone was happy. Except the regular Discord members who no longer had access to meta discussions from some of the most invested and knowledgeable players. We tried discussing with staff a few times to find a solution, but nothing came of it and it’s basically unsalvageable at this point.

At that point, I was still in the mindset of “I’ll probably go back once everyone calms down.” But, a few weeks later, they removed Pecan (the one who made the skill bashin charts for CMs) and myself as Helpers due to inactivity. This is something that had no precedent (previously Helpers disappeared for months without any result) and also no warning. It felt like such a petty slap in the face. I basically decided not to return at that point. At worst they were doing it to spite us, at best they were doing it to try to bait us to return and do free labour. Some other Helpers tried to talk to staff about the decision, but again, staff wouldn’t change their mind.

There have been more instances of staff doing things I find distasteful but this is long enough already and the above covers the main reasons. The other things simply reinforced my decision to stay away. I have a place to chat where I’m more comfortable now.

Resting raises Energy. Dating raises Mood. Racing raises Fans. Training raises Stats.

If you suspend the run, you can come back to it later. If you abandon it, it’s gone and you have to start a new one.

Helpful Tools
-------------

Gametora Training Event Helper
------------------------------

[https://gametora.com/umamusume/training-event-helper](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://gametora.com/umamusume/training-event-helper&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375665841&usg=AOvVaw1vx9W9fZIFeobLAXngHQDq)

Enter the cards and uma you’re using into this site and you can use it to check what all the conversation options in the game will give you.

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztboeSI3QFuz1RViYThv3gr6B_NuRZJL2xUsFejbG_HztNbY0xxGBz6pgIv2hXVZDIBdcZxN1sBLvU25TFHra9s5RTiQeNCMk6CBu8ThudyEQTYkNKKbMylh1_h4c7b2q9ujH8jtivc1ILcjMKEUG73s29zrciGisVUmww=s600)

Match the text in the top left of the screen in-game when there’s a choice to the options here, then click the option to see what each choice gives you.

Using this will help you make stronger umas, as the choices you make in all of the events that happen during a run are quite significant. In general, try to pick stats from events that you don’t normally train, such as Stamina or Intelligence in a Speed/Power build. Energy and Motivation are also very important when you need them, and sometimes a conversation will even give you a hint for a useful skill.

Tip: If you get a choice between 10 Energy or 30 Energy and a possible sickness, choose the 30 Energy. It succeeds almost every time, at least 80% of the time if not more.

Keyboard Controls for DMM
-------------------------

This is an Auto Hotkey script for the DMM version of the game that allows you to do training runs using your keyboard. If you get RSI from using a mouse, it may help. The game doesn’t track clicks, so it’s safe to use currently.

Download AHK from here: [https://www.autohotkey.com/download/](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://www.autohotkey.com/download/&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375668005&usg=AOvVaw30RpZVZctsR4BkYOTbnkVZ) 

Then save this script as a .ahk file and run it as administrator: [https://pastebin.com/MuerUj20](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://pastebin.com/MuerUj20&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375668269&usg=AOvVaw1Bj_ZGSUHmKdme-kVgX7BW)

During training, you can use QWE and ASD to interact with the two rows of buttons. For example, W goes to training. 12345 will select the different trainings, while \` will go back.

The RFV column is for races. F signs up for the race, R confirms it, then V skips the race. Space goes through the rest of the buttons, so you can spam that until you’re back. Space is also used for the inheritance event, though many buttons will manage to click it.

The TGB column is for conversations. T picks the top option, G picks the bottom option. If there are three options, B will select the very top option. You can change any of these by altering the letters before the :: in the AHK script. For selecting skills or changing races, it’s best to go back to the mouse.

Here’s a picture that may or may not help you understand.

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztYZeKW7Ac-n9Eiv_d11p7fLdLubUBMoM0xQnZCnrVwBtUnXizKKRUo8DlzvVzfdz2L2VF7IR9i--Qfv0TtbTa4JLyt-nDRtIk80k79mZBADw4lo2XlovSiQ9F2_yRd17lhCFZpUtlgKyM3QqpEA_4GVhOqc-rwMlHWE5Q=s600)

Automatic Screenshot Combiner
-----------------------------

[https://ssc.kitachan.black/](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://ssc.kitachan.black/&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375670009&usg=AOvVaw371OKnaUu3gS9Ca2bHU8P4) 

Here, you can paste multiple screenshots of an uma screen and get a stitched together image. It’s useful for sharing your builds or parents. If you’re playing on Windows, use Alt+Print Screen to capture the image with your skills/factors scrolled all the way to the top, then Ctrl+V to paste it into the site. Repeat those steps with the list scrolled down. Repeat until you have screenshots of everything. Then click the purple button on the site to get the combined image.

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztbbVNUUoTGOo1auRynu-QupIV8NEPx-jSb-_zyPqd1a6csNxj_nUHZnDAdJB4QXvBVlGjhiJ_geT6Q3JaJGNiA2wo5BomA4--D2JQH_lsiHM5qoi_0R7IAznPyrddH038F6t0mmHw-c2H7ISs8AsTs__A_GBWI_PG_f6Zs=s600)

Putting those three in and pressing the button results in this: [https://cdn.discordapp.com/attachments/391257802347118594/1028059556116451328/unknown.png](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://cdn.discordapp.com/attachments/391257802347118594/1028059556116451328/unknown.png&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375671165&usg=AOvVaw3YjDgC_ogmguyJL_MKzR5G) 

The extra options are, in order, “Cut off the top” (eg, the DMM bar), “Cut off the left/right edges,” “Crop out the uma information (i.e. just show factors/skills),” “Remove the scroll bars,” “Leave the bottom of the final image,” and the final option is something you can try if the images don’t merge properly.

Learning Japanese with Uma
--------------------------

Trying to learn Japanese through the game with no foundation at all will be pretty painful. However, you can use it as motivation and supplement. Just be careful if you’re male, considering all the characters are girls. Copying them may garner you weird looks.

You are going to need to learn kanji at some point. For a free option, [Koohii](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://kanji.koohii.com/&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375672540&usg=AOvVaw20w3tDLdHs_GUyVEABc69e) is quite good. For paid, there’s [WaniKani](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://www.wanikani.com/&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375672681&usg=AOvVaw2vMV2bv4ND003sd2Zi_YxV). These both used spaced repetition systems to help you remember. Knowing even just the English meaning of kanji is very helpful. For example, 飛行機. If you know the meanings, you can see it’s “fly journey machine.” Airplane. Not all make quite that much sense, but it does make words easier to remember. Even though irony (皮肉) is “skin meat” that’s a pretty memorable oddity.

For lessons and vocab, for free, there’s [Renshuu](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://www.renshuu.org&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375673375&usg=AOvVaw3tGFzNPXxURwHo8vMB6bnw). Use the settings to remove the reading vectors from Kanji tests. For paid, I like [NativShark](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://www.nativshark.com/&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375673593&usg=AOvVaw28PTF4USVYd9N3Anl5E-Jr) for its native audio and smooth experience. If you want to speak accurately, [Dogen’s pronunciation lessons](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://www.patreon.com/posts/japanese-index-16489306&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375673861&usg=AOvVaw3LCCbm1T_ZNQUoQHq9yWYH) are great.

As for where to start in Uma, I made [a spreadsheet here](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://docs.google.com/spreadsheets/d/1sScxyshiTqqRE9NMlx3ztSeuZKHcVDgiQYbFRfXrdDk/edit%23gid%3D0&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375674164&usg=AOvVaw1E24EmFVwDjzpPiv0L7-Jz) that uses various metrics to determine the readability of the uma’s character stories (the ones with 7 chapters). jReadability is the most modern of the metrics and generally is regarded as being most accurate. You can use this to judge where to start or who to avoid until you’re more experienced.

All of the characters in that sheet have a link to [japanese.io](https://www.google.com/url?q=http://japanese.io&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375674843&usg=AOvVaw11mejRw_fsJRFfXkjpZNGT) where you can read that story with assisted reading capabilities. If a story has differences for male and female trainers, the stories there will be the female version. If you hover kanji, it will show the furigana. If you click a word, it will show the definition. This can be a crutch so you might not want to rely on it heavily, but the site has some cool features. Read alongside the game so you can hear the lines being spoken.

When reading, you will find many things you don’t know. You can check [Jisho](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://jisho.org/&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375675682&usg=AOvVaw3grhoFaGnswjz0Mo5ZUT72) for the definitions of words, if you’re not using japanese.io. [Tae Kim’s Grammar Guide](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://guidetojapanese.org/learn/grammar&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375675894&usg=AOvVaw3Us44Fo8H_b4gUlw73lqql) is good for grammar. [JLPT Sensei](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://jlptsensei.com/learn-japanese-grammar/%25e3%2581%25a9%25e3%2581%2586%25e3%2581%2597%25e3%2581%25a6-doushite-meaning/&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375676115&usg=AOvVaw1cH6CUovP2nFKl9mBteRKr) also has a lot of good info. You can usually find the page by just googling [it + “jlpt sensei”](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://duckduckgo.com/?va%3Dj%26t%3Dhb%26q%3Dkurai%2Bjlpt%2Bsensei%26ia%3Dweb&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375676430&usg=AOvVaw3eOlnMVjA5nf98nW51yzNH). If you can’t find anything at all, you can ask on [HiNative](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://hinative.com/&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375676654&usg=AOvVaw2tzN7WUZbcGqP7nz8dCW0-), just make sure to answer some questions about English in return.

As you find new words or phrases, you might want to put them into an [Anki](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://apps.ankiweb.net/&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375677198&usg=AOvVaw1oXhD5zXdGb4v3_mRdADbp) deck to help you remember them. (If you use NativShark, you can make custom flashcards there which will be the same thing basically) Using the app every day, it will show you words with a spaced repetition system to help you remember, just like Koohii or WaniKani. [This plugin](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://ankiweb.net/shared/info/907064139&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375677800&usg=AOvVaw1WDX3lemKzUHFMcck84aF1) makes it easier for Anki. Japanese.io also has a button on the side (looking like a thumbpin) that allows you to make a flashcard deck for a text, though it’s a paid feature.

You can also find premade Anki decks on [AnkiWeb](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://ankiweb.net/shared/decks/japanese&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375678523&usg=AOvVaw1pFsA8_JJPW6HtD9uy5qUF). These are put up by users, so the quality may not be the greatest, but you can use them as a starting point. Anki is great for helping you remember words, kanji, or phrases, but it’s no replacement for an actual curated course.

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztZPT_yXGnbd1HPEVUHgK6w8e_vElWZf0yIQINk-WF6YR4jV9eIjnuv-3U0PvBG-ZPh09OCeoPdGronPztOAVQb2pAgqqcXFBpguo-ROnTO1QGuEEtI3n9u-yerQ_JD79y7iFA6k0qKw2hW0T0lD6UUHMz-ik5AWIm_Wdlw=s600)

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztY4d5KYGrwEhEZ5LJigtosHYLtYPCUPT2j6143weqbZsEdaeOF9gIYtZHyYPXdyvdSVcILrvOgTwAdKJbUNdynmoEu6IzxXJUrgcm1AnRLez0MNEss5RLwj7qq3YWJWcmzQk9tM6bq5hADduqRacG1rnBTe6pzN5Byj624=s600)

If you want to check your work, you can see the translations from the English patch [here](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://github.com/noccu/umamusu-translate/tree/master/translations/story/04&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375679709&usg=AOvVaw0KswSixAZsG4NLvTImohg6). Each folder is labelled with the uma’s game id number, which you can see on [GameTora](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://gametora.com/umamusume/characters&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375680188&usg=AOvVaw36010ys2habGtuYeQF4RRd). Just choose “Sort by: Game ID number” and remove the last two numbers, which represent the costume. For example, Mejiro Bright is 1074. The folder contains the seven chapters, and then you can see the JP and English side by side. Scroll down bit by bit as you read and see how close you were. Though, note that [many of these](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://github.com/noccu/umamusu-translate/blob/master/tl-progress.md%23character-stories&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375681068&usg=AOvVaw2f4HsVa89mlaC4CmyzbjJI) are [machine translated](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://www.deepl.com/translator&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375681260&usg=AOvVaw02ESuItpN_2m1ViYlc9z1r), so may not be perfectly accurate. Maybe try contributing when you feel confident. You can also use the jpText lines here to allow you to copy/paste the words you see to look them up. \\n is a linebreak.

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztaNSqiNxlaQktFMSWZ9or7KgxCHPMkKaeotrxrSTfFGOkhgbmAQK8w2FM2OwZfwwL1KJtpXKRYGHcS0fNqRJGsK_zFK8pDrXhbw6Y4hxZ9NQ_0-EKAVrqcRU9Z81vzlWtUjJffaGt7l4U43ZUGHLWrjFFwXkalhIRg3jN8=s600)

Note that using the English patch is against the game’s terms of service. If you decide to apply it to your game, you do so at your own risk. Nobody has been banned for it yet, but Cygames could change that policy at any time without warning.

Now, there are many times you will have no idea what something is even with these tools. This happens a lot with umas like Rickey, Helios, Rudolf, and others who use specific speech. For Rickey, it’s Chinese Feng Shui. For Helios, Gyaru language. For Rudolf, strange sayings. Even people fluent in Japanese have no idea what these umas are saying at times.

Let’s say you’re reading Rickey’s story, and you see this:

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuzta3MA5DEbCDPPKFoTM3Wg8CAQIRADv9YqgBqE-l2PS9A76rG7CmM6qj2RnlL54BEzG_qVqGw5ha2Fqk_cGMHqIn_d4CcFN6WESUcwqwrrv2yiOt2dSoNhcGiE4nDOYTpaGY7fOtrWTShaWD_hYgjNMTpamQa6VvuWmCZAE=s600)

The first part is easy enough. “キミ” is “you,” “と” is “and,” “私” is “I,” “まさしく” is “certainly.” But what’s the last part? We are certainly what? You check Jisho, but all that comes up is:

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztb9xz1hGbt_CXVdVQ4Q8JOZCb8J8hvs05RLbwr_7NgHkz5bUGL2S-uOBxnBF9F9dLSVoA9hpaWdjEYRrFFizew6PMa6fLruVawoQw1llNsLPkguPMOhc9fB-0thc3wiA9qrqpEzQ4JHKU_tcR0CisOnx57AK3jc0fJ1zH0=s600)

The second step is checking a Japanese-Japanese dictionary, and then putting that into a translator. If you have a Mac, you can enable Super Daijirin in the Dictionary app, an extremely good one. For others, a shortcut is googling “<term>ってなに”. (“What is <term>?”)

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztbAHonxwx2ENhyYW-TvEjNEQI6vfpQ88A8SPj-Yokv0bQpmH5PP9kTBFZhQDqr9e6HHuhRof-x70nsYJ3YRnui9TW_4qpHZLkdz_A_xEY1EYFDcplRjJ4T60s_c0XKwqyJzIUEMhcd3Pp_9c-0EIrSI7Cfxt90v3Oxu=s600)

Then you can put that into DeepL, which will say: “Compatibility. In the [five elements theory](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wuxing_\(Chinese_philosophy\)&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375685870&usg=AOvVaw30KdIgrxKygE0K1C_JkvRX), fire comes from wood, earth from fire, gold from earth, water from gold, and wood from water.”

Unsurprisingly, it’s more feng shui nonsense. Wikipedia translates it as “mutual generation.” Thanks Rickey. Glad I’m learning so much. For Rickey specifically, you can also try putting the kanji into a [Chinese dictionary](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://www.mdbg.net/chinese/dictionary?page%3Dworddict%26wdrst%3D1%26wdqb%3D%25E7%259B%25B8%25E7%2594%259F&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375686942&usg=AOvVaw1DaCfwdG9neM03SC0NA6uz). For Helios, even googling in Japanese won’t save you…

Gacha
-----

The SSR/3\* rates in Uma Musume are 3%, with rate-up cards and umas appearing 0.75% of the time usually. They sometimes change this, but in general, you’ll get the rate-up thing ¼ times you get an SSR/3\*. This is the same whether there are two rate-ups or only one. Each rate-up will be 0.75%, it doesn’t go up to 1.5% for single rate-ups.

You also get to pick any of the rate-up things after doing 200 pulls, which is known as sparking. To get MLB SSRs, you will on average need to spark twice.

There are currently no limited umas. You can pull any character, even the summer or Christmas ones, at any time. Some welfare support cards are event-exclusive, but otherwise there are also no limited support cards.

Here’s a table showing what you can expect from certain numbers of pulls, sparks included, for any given rate-up.

SSR


|Pulls|None|0LB|1LB|2LB |3LB |MLB |
|-----|----|---|---|----|----|----|
|50   |69% |26%|5% |0.6%|0.1%|0%  |
|100  |47% |36%|13%|3%  |0.6%|0.1%|
|200  |0%  |22%|34%|25% |13% |6%  |
|300  |0%  |11%|24%|27% |20% |19% |
|400  |0%  |0% |5% |15% |22% |58% |
|500  |0%  |0% |2% |9%  |17% |72% |
|600  |0%  |0% |0% |1%  |5%  |94% |


Sometimes, there will be an uma banner with no rate up. This happened for New Year’s 2021. In this case, you can spark any uma. Note that if you’re spending money on this, the spark is around 600 dollars, while the [Blu-Rays](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://gametora.com/umamusume/merchandise&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375703406&usg=AOvVaw3btGzsZPp1WQWbeiSDZ_Wd) are cheaper and give you a pick ticket for any uma up to Meisho Doto. (Plus 2000 [statues](#h.8jnmjs3xvfdw) to get the uma to 5 stars, and some useless SSRs) If the uma you want is pre-Doto, then you can get her cheaper by buying those than buying a spark.

Training Pass
-------------

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztYVys7rfrtmPQOF-8iKVfSJJR3MRRXZxrZ1FyWHSwQ4Mj8PmroQ5fySAYjexxGiq4R7YRcljgAh_axjVJtpPQCAI6B1s3KM_m4sQKJPAlNgM50yfxrXaTn6P_wJ_VGI2naLL0eoXPvMQBECkHsOPTOK19dpjMtAX8KELeQ=s600)

The training pass resets monthly with a standard free version and a premium paid version. Purchasing the premium version comes with 350 paid jewels. Points are added per turn during training runs, where stats are counted at 100%, skill points at 50%, and 5% of the sum of displayed stats upon completing a training run. All values are rounded up.

Checkpoint rewards grant a number of in-game resources, tickets, and jewels. Here’s a table of the important stuff upon clearing the pass.


|Item                    |Standard|Premium|Total|
|------------------------|--------|-------|-----|
|Support card tickets    |5       |20     |25   |
|Free jewels             |500     |1350   |1850 |
|Uma gacha tickets       |2       |2      |4    |
|Support gacha tickets   |2       |2      |4    |
|Rainbow crystal fragment|0       |1      |1    |


Training Umas
-------------

The umas you pull from the gachas are not completed umas. They are merely templates, which you can use to make clones of that uma to then train into something that can race. You can make these templates stronger by increasing their star count or level. Doing so won’t increase the strength of already-trained umas.

When you go to train an uma, you’ll be greeted with this screen at first, where you select the uma you want to train.

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztZz5zZbDBm5OQc3RlQlC_Gmug2_eOltx5Zu1Kxvg3z9LTxMU8HmTFlpnmM_0OZVeoEtRijX5yDfgt1Zu5Xfg4IkDl3vBm_bjXRmYPoK-YPhszI9L7hv4jt7vzkdAIbH-c5cnAQJenB65UkauP3XbKWTV9Q1SB76i_M=s600)

Umas with a golden border are level 3 or higher, which is raised by trading [shoes and banners](#h.5gfcd5kzoxm). Stars can be raised by trading pieces, which are acquired by pulling duplicates from the gacha, trading statues in the shop, and various other things that aren’t consistently available. At three stars, an uma unlocks her unique racing outfit and gains the ability to pass down her unique skill through inheritance. Skills at three stars are also notably stronger than their 1 or 2 star counterparts.

Selecting an uma won’t consume that uma. It makes a fresh copy. Every time you do a training run, you’ll start with a fresh clone of that uma with the template’s base stats.

Inheritance
-----------

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuzta6C72SGObr8LafYHiiOTDpOQ2D-QSsOYrc0o3ATAHyIu8aS2PL7Qy452_k19EuO69Rje4TSZ7ptvrK242dUzqo7kXxIuHVgDR3cXcZEsPQpsfkuSpxao03bttqmJ4rOMoY3ngCoFV_NOlGrsgr_W4QJ-lvAZWX8p9HaQ=s600)

After selecting an uma, you’ll be presented with the inheritance screen. Here, you’ll select umas to, as the game puts it, “send feelings” to the uma you’re raising. Most people simply refer to these as the uma’s parents.

Whenever you finish a run with an uma, that uma will receive certain inheritance factors roughly related to her own abilities. The uma’s actual stats and skills don’t matter at all for inheritance, only these inheritance factors.

You can also borrow umas from people you follow to use as parents.

If you press and hold on a trained uma, it will bring up their stats page. On the second tab, you can see the inheritance factors they and their parents have. All of these factors can be passed down to the uma you’re training.

As an example, let’s look at the inheritance factors one of my umas has. It’s a Tokai Teio, who had Oguri Cap and Rice Shower as her parents.

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztYgtNa_x0Rii_gQOxLoa9IPhGEFZiVHZUEDEIbMhb2lxmMMe1az73WQzJWpvCLowORJjc0crGSTNbrEtNBkgCCwCvvydDhj6E7_z3WVb-FRBOHOw5ddTJTNs3jgIC2NIEdVzhX5x1PyvfC_Eu5PLXZ5G0qHhuMy-KRyrw=s600)

Blue factors are stat inheritance. The Teio and Oguri both have 3 star Speed inheritance factors. The Rice Shower has a 3 star Stamina factor. A 1 star factor gives +5 to that stat, a 2 star factor gives +12, and a 3 star gives +21. So, this uma will give +42 Speed and +21 Stamina. We’d refer to her as a 6\* Speed 3\* Stamina uma.

Pink factors are aptitude factors. They’ll raise the letter grade of the related aptitudes, in this case, Long Distance, Turf, and Middle Distance. You can’t raise them above A at the start, but during the two inheritance events, they might get upgraded to S if there’s a related pink factor in the heritage.

The green and gray factors are for skills and races. Skill factors will give hints for the skill of the same name, while [race factors](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://gametora.com/umamusume/g1-race-factor-list&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375717169&usg=AOvVaw02z3Lt5LEUiSakek24IqTJ) will give bonus stats. For example, the URAシナリオ factor can give +10 Speed/Stamina for each star if it activates during an inheritance event.

Let’s see how putting this Teio as a parent changes things.

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztZKSVG6d-VAP3bTwrvbIvWdeAQmVKKSdCshNrq41APOGJnm3H_h5Q5v780o0umh9VCtHeaJxN9IUOu2ZLMayosXZCZ-Il8fgE2SQptn7ZXyRMheFgi8BqWWCMtU_NbN_Mwv6YTzyf6QGzb78c1BRdbhYv1StyX7Fr6cdQ=s600)

As expected, it added 42 Speed and 21 Stamina. Teio’s Long Distance pink factor and Rice Shower’s Middle Distance pink factor raised those distances from G to F. This uma already has an A in Turf, so Oguri’s Turf pink factor doesn’t do anything, but if it gets triggered during an inheritance event, the Turf aptitude will be raised to S.

The stats of the parent umas don’t matter at all. An A rank uma and a C rank uma with the same inheritance factors will both give exactly the same inheritance.

One other thing to look out for when selecting inheritance is the compatibility, shown in the top right.

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztYjWfg6oePpVaz5BYklzc1MMo3-c85XgmtAzT1_50xyMFTTvz-uqfPqJ_EJtGJjmPMqxh1JLn0Ydt6O_qQbOXGta1ZtLC268BPw3qQmz4UKDHvPQ2HY_8YOVAsuNwXa8TGJ6UDBOViMu_tEGzOSDEqIBfbLTKotacc=s600)![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztYkRTF6osK5ud0j6CeC6yGQOw7ZxzX-8FWN4Qj7tNJFUC7urhSuPGs8p9qHStxESRziz5Q5PzdDPSTtDBSBOfTNFPVGgMp3q-lwIEuH8wTCm_ed7XOrEy_lUu0h9wocUXt84KtIYZKY4ErCwUIiMmtc_wQWBSuL14dDUQ=s600)![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztaz02CAHEPbeULyO1n7YaY5Ib7ApTitj-8TwVwP_XtHNzKDlTiTYj2vofpSCKsxVg9d97nOsBvsB45KGJIeHeRcr5rLGN-bzafbm201Jv8KesI-ZLDmbkvVtKF_LZgtvTcTfgyTuXQlqYcMGEYPT0GEXCJeus8LrJFEmw=s600)

A triangle means there’s bad compatibility. A circle means there’s good compatibility. Two circles means there’s great compatibility. There are no discrete differences between each icon for the chances of inheriting factors.

The better the compatibility, the higher the chance of getting a lot of inherited factors during the two inheritance events during the training, which happen in early April. Aim for a double circle if you can, but it’s not very important. To check the best parents for an uma, use [GameTora’s compatibility calculator](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://gametora.com/umamusume/compatibility&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375719850&usg=AOvVaw1shIo7N6I3nH84yFAgCukL). Choose your desired uma, then click Recommend to see the best.

Calculating compatibility
-------------------------

Compatibility within a single parent slot is calculated by adding the target uma compatibility with the parent, and two instances of hidden shared attributes between the target uma, parent, and grandparent. The total compatibility based only on uma is the sum of both parent slots and the compatibility of the parents with each other.

Additional compatibility can be obtained through mutual G1 wins which count for 3 points each. There are five separate instances where race wins are counted. Between a parent and one of its grandparents for a total of two instances within a parent slot (a grand total of four when both slots are filled), and one instance across parent slots.

Say we wanted to double circle (151 points) with one parent slot for Suzuka using a Dober parent with Digital and Oguri grandparents, we would need at least an additional 97 points.

Single circle        23 + 16 + 15 + 3(18 +13) = 147

Double circle        23 + 16 + 15 + 3(20 + 14) = 156

In this case, the difference is a win in Hanshin JF (Digital) and February Stakes (both).

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztZmuHAzEwxeZS3MBVLM35RPO3a62krdAuklXz9jQJnUJDIisUf0-RYhgT0UHE-Oa6S-ChmXYyuGJ5tlaM9EWiI9c2w3PknNmcJLCT0wRthAbbE2kpOUgEHqpjG4ENcmZ1y_z9BZqcEPymFlT9PROHdC4Djj5MQulANQzg8=s600)

Here is a calculated example for double circling Urara who has the lowest sum compatibility across the board with every existing uma by additional race compatibility.

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztYNf_7VkTlOeLYGi67s2wRMVTXHk7Sx3J0uBfykPS2HZoH-gm2uLg6zDYdeaEhzaZUgN1dwuVN9XjGYFQN7M1_t-kKPgW108qMQNakOMPfkkDjeYjzz9vaOkwXaaaTv7IYEJniV2bQJGSs3jGxHS8l2ZIY5LnB6Y1Z7QkI=s600)

9 (Compatibility between Urara and Dober)

0 (Shared attributes between Urara, Dober, and Digital)

0 (Shared attributes between Urara, Dober, and Oguri)

1 (Compatibility between Urara and Ines)

1 (Shared attributes between Urara, Ines, and Seiun)

0 (Shared attributes between Urara, Ines, and El)

24 (Compatibility between Dober and Ines)

The compatibility purely from uma is 9 + 1 + 1 + 24 = 35.

20 (Mutual wins between Dober and Digital)

14 (Mutual wins between Dober and Oguri)

2 (Mutual wins between Ines and Seiun)

2 (Mutual wins between Ines and El)

1 (Mutual wins between Dober and Ines)

Our final compatibility is now 35 + 3(20 + 14 + 2 + 2 + 1) = 152

3 Star Blue Factor Chance
-------------------------

After finishing a run, the uma gets random inheritance factors to pass down to its future children. The blue factor is chosen randomly between the five stats, and then the stars are chosen based on how high that stat is. The pink factor is chosen randomly between all aptitudes the uma has at A or higher. Here’s a chart of the chance to get a 3 star factor based on what the stat is.


|Stat Value   |<600|600~1100|>1100|
|-------------|----|--------|-----|
|1 Star Factor|~90%|~50%    |~20% |
|2 Star Factor|~10%|~45%    |~70% |
|3 Star Factor|0%  |~6%     |~10% |


After finishing an uma, a stat is randomly chosen, and then the stars for that stat are chosen. So, if you had 1200 Speed, the chance of getting a 3 star Speed factor would be 20% (the chance of it picking Speed) \* 10% (the chance of it being 3 star) for a total of ~2%. It doesn’t matter if you win the run or not.

Raising Aptitude
----------------

During the initial inheritance, the amount the aptitude increases is based on the number of total stars among the pink factors of the parents and grandparents. Raising it by one rank requires 1 star, then each subsequent rank requires another three stars. You can raise each aptitude a total of four ranks in this way, so an F rank aptitude could only go to B during the initial inheritance, even with 18 total stars.

As an example, Mayano Top Gun has an E rank Dirt aptitude and a D rank Mile aptitude. In order to raise her for Dirt PvP, you would need to raise both to A. Getting Mile to C takes one star, then 3 to get to B, and 3 more to get to A, for a total of 7. The Dirt aptitude requires one more step, so it requires 10 total stars.

In total, this is 17, but it’s not possible to do all at the start since one uma can’t have two different pink aptitudes. You could do 7 Mile and 9 Dirt, or 6 Mile and 10 Dirt. One of them will have to be left at B and raised through an inheritance event.

Chance of Inheriting Factors
----------------------------

The chance of inheriting factors is influenced by your individual affinity with each uma and whether the uma is a parent or grandparent. Here’s a table I stole from [Crazyfellow](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://docs.google.com/document/d/1Q3IJKbtkplmuY-PAJMNjYiLtasv0eU0aIBEqp8_C3tg/edit?tab%3Dt.0&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375730481&usg=AOvVaw3nkcMxcV9VoCZr5T0QQ9yb).


|Factor type↓ / Base chance→|☆1 Rate|☆2 Rate|☆3 Rate|
|---------------------------|-------|-------|-------|
|Blue                       |70%    |80%    |90%    |
|Pink                       |1%     |3%     |5%     |
|Green (Unique Skill)       |5%     |10%    |15%    |
|Race Whites                |1%     |2%     |3%     |
|Other Whites               |3%     |6%     |9%     |


These numbers are then increased by a % equalling your affinity. So, if you had 30 affinity with your parent that had a 3 star green on their unique, you would have a 15% \* 1.3 = ~20% chance of inheriting it instead of 15%.

Grandparents have their inheritance rates halved (though I’d imagine not for blues).

Chance of Getting White Factors
-------------------------------

When you finish a run, you have a chance to get white factors for each skill you have. If it’s a white skill, it’s a 20% chance. If it’s a ◎ skill, it’s 25%. If it’s a gold skill, it’s 40%. For each parent or grandparent that also has the spark, you add 2.5%, or 5% for the gold skills.

Other white factors, such as race and scenario sparks, follow the same pattern, counting as white skills (so 20% base chance).

For stars, it’s 50% for 1 star, 45% for 2 stars, and 5% for 3 stars. These change to 20% for 1 star, 70% for 2 star, and 10% for 3 star if the uma is SS rank or higher.

Finding Friends
---------------

The best site to find friends is Uma Musume DB. There’s also Gamewith and Djeeta, but their functionality is lacking.

[https://uma.pure-db.com/#/search](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://uma.pure-db.com/%23/search&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375737235&usg=AOvVaw26ybVAgRk0gB-ZKihNo0XF) 

You can set it to English by clicking the Language selector in the top right. The site is fairly self-explanatory. The only noteworthy thing I’ll point out is that before searching, you can switch between Representative Factor and All. If you’re searching for a parent for breeding purposes, Representative Factor is very useful to find umas with 3\* Blue, Pink, and Scenario factors.

GameWith
--------

[https://gamewith.jp/uma-musume/article/show/260740](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://gamewith.jp/uma-musume/article/show/260740&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375738285&usg=AOvVaw2EBAozBSFDeO41a640XxFL)

Scroll down until you see this button, then click it:

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztYUyFNyQ9HCW9ipWDT428TFcOU1RRaKF5MNQriuHRUWVJYaQwKVB6h6ZcNKp6or-fPhzYeArKhinVgp0zLYjbDYNhvaw41kt7rkKfyE2YcFky5-taosC_T5fQ8UoUeHkXtUV-YKcu7lCjEbl9cZ78KU0qkx71Md_Rr4DQ=s600)

It will bring up the filter section.

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztbGUGKSW-ubIX3EUxCKUQmD-ZDe0AVIikr4g_k4aajA66IaRmJiFy7G0jyfOkQttKC1ZxYwBYZT-ozzCV15UN5XGw1rbbjnZFlq4nvd6HgHVWo_CbpZ-l9gG5cnE4RD1SD18tm3d2P8fktN8Q-pZw7iJu9u6TEKWXtacg=s600)

Input what you’re searching for, then scroll through the list looking for the best one. I tend to load a bunch of pages, then use Ctrl+F to find specific traits.

DjeetaSearch
------------

[https://djeeta-search.com/index.html](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://djeeta-search.com/index.html&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375739355&usg=AOvVaw04W5axhG1U02H2p7tXROhJ)

Click “Friend search” in the top bar. The first tab lets you specify the support card you want, the second tab is for the uma and parents, and the third tab is for inheritance factors.

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztYrP-8k1wQJjV97VQiUBzwSb8TSZ9KO7bs0gGm979iO6mCrHGqS5UXbXrTozWGqcwVCRhtX49Oa7AUjPWQ_lRuw_jRJM_UGmILB88nu17Gmg2qzBxpU48hO85Mgczy2lpO3f1KaRw8ooWBQoK_PGr_nXYJzxuLjTjPsmg=s600)

Friends to Look For
-------------------

The JP server has very high quality parents these days. You can be quite picky.

*   For [Champions Meeting](#h.n319dua1dy0z), you’ll want to look for umas with strong uniques for the track. The guides should tell you who these are. Then, also get 9\* distance that matches the track. Look for high race counts for better compatibility and lots of whites to get skill hints. When you learn more about the game, you can look for specific white factors for the track, such as the speed greens and certain white skills that your cards don’t give you.
*   For [Stadium](#h.ffg71a6svcim), you’ll generally want to inherit whatever stat has the highest cap, to inflate your uma’s rank. In DYI, that’s Speed. The inheritance will raise your cap, letting you get a bunch of rank points, which increases your Stadium score. For the uma, Summer Gold Ship is great, as her unique can trigger one of your golds, giving more consistency. Find them for each distance if you can.
*   There are various white factors ending in の遺伝子, like 短距離の遺伝子. On uma pure, these are in the “Other” category. If they get inherited, they will give you S, just like the reds do. You can add this for your distance to the search to improve consistency. Anything with シナリオ in the name will also probably be useful.
*   Of course, you’ll also want to find friends with the cards you want to borrow, such as the friend card for the scenario you’re playing.

Adding Friends
--------------

Open the menu by clicking the button in the top right of the home screen.

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztYwdOwF-gU176yV2Sdn0LGh0ErhUW-yktkyH2uDe_TQXIuarAWlNHjhULqGaz1tg0Fz9zXZAH-1IWVnoWd-dpfnhJokBAZ3KMGD6ExZlD1RvJOHrGDGGA_eLIawakL0f911jA6s1hsYED3H2zlJjQ7kV5W9ddcopdU=s600)

After entering the ID, you’ll see a button like this.

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztZLku5oRU7Kf72WCApwLYFYOnuyWqo2qdwJ73jT96F_KGC7tr2oi9p8oRS8tqglIeLr1PkBM2qrPB5i59WK9qHhYlOuAXDE4BK38ktCyMGcOMI7XXhNOJ2AuhN9OLudSK1Y2eRW_TleBz74tEbU5byx3GoTutTpQiLq4w=s600)

If the button is gray, either you’re capped on follows, or the person has reached the follower cap. You can unfollow someone if it’s the former. In the middle part of the first image, the follow count can be seen at the bottom left, which for me is 12/13. If you’re not capped, then that means they are, and you’ll have to find someone else.

Support Card Restrictions
-------------------------

There are a few different reasons why you might not be able to use a support card.

![](https://docs.google.com/docs-images-rt/ALKuztav74xiSnyIQl-VghmpoHZA5Hgxq1oZTLmpSxO2WTjGjSbc19Me6PRrg3VW6774TamPjKVSI349CgpOrTVyj-nROLoa0rzPJ59nBRTDGEwcQbl3wqFeBmBP3c93mXcR_U_1Lif0PF8ZV68I19Uq1MY8-EE6zmx0F-0x-g=s600)

The message above the first support card is saying that you can’t use it while training that uma. Since the uma being trained is Tokai Teio, I cannot use Tokai Teio’s support card.

The message above the third support card is saying that another support card of the same uma is present. The deck already contains the Power version of Nishino, so I can’t add the Speed version of Nishino.

The messages above the other cards are simply saying that the card is already present in the deck.

Currencies
----------

Jewels (Carrots)
----------------

Jewels are used for many things, primarily for rolling on the gacha. You can also use them to refill your energy or race points. There are paid jewels and free jewels. Paid jewels can be used for the daily pull that costs 50 jewels. If a source takes either paid jewels or free jewels, it will take free jewels first.

Non-Renewable Sources

Missions - Translated [here](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://gametora.com/umamusume/missions&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375745313&usg=AOvVaw2CktpsWu7Wme0gJkyU0qSn).

Trophies - Each G3 or above race you win for the first time will give you jewels. There are 30 G1 for 100 each, 36 G2 for 50 each, and 69 G3 for 30 each, a total of 6870 jewels. Refer to [this list](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://gametora.com/umamusume/races&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375745745&usg=AOvVaw0gUuFaUTfVNHSBPsb5XHUo) to see when they are.

Levels - Every level up gives you 50 jewels. You get EXP from talking to umas, seeing their events in the training runs, training umas, and a bunch of other random things. Check your level in the [Uma Directory](#h.xqe6w96boc31) now and then to collect any jewels you might have received.

[Stories](#h.wnsy64egwuzg) - As you raise your bond with umas, you’ll unlock more of their stories. Reading them rewards gems. The first four give 20 jewels each, while the last three give 50 jewels each. You can read the first four stories of an uma during their banner, so make sure to check it out for the free 80 jewels. There’re also the main stories which also unlock free SSR cards.

Shop Deals - There’s a 1500 paid jewels package you can buy twice, and a 7500 paid jewels package you can buy three times. These get refreshed from time to time.

DMM Login - The first time you log in through DMM, you’ll receive 500 jewels.

Renewable Sources

Daily Missions - 50 jewels per day.

Daily Login Bonus - 110 jewels per week.

Weekly [Stadium](#h.4tonsji7onb5) Rewards - Up to 250 jewels per week.

Monthly [Circle](#h.q5k0974asyw) Rewards - Up to 3000 jewels per month, but more realistically 150~1200.

[Champion’s Meeting](#h.n319dua1dy0z) - Up to 3600 jewels per month, if you win everything in Graded League, or up to 1940 for winning everything in Open League.

[Legend Races](#h.y41gl2j47oqc) - Winning gives 250 jewels each.

[Story Events](#h.d6xwv6h5u7k4) - Usually one per month, unless there’s a different event. 450 jewels from the bingo cards, 1500 from the event points, and 210 from the stories gives 2160 jewels total.

Monthly Pass - Gives you 500 paid jewels immediately, then 50 free jewels per day for a month.

Note that many things also give pink tickets, which can be counted as 150 jewels for their respective banner. For example, Story Events give two of each, and winning Graded A CM gives five of each. There’s also usually [mission sets](https://www.google.com/url?q=https://gametora.com/umamusume/missions/history-2022&sa=D&source=editors&ust=1790844375750076&usg=AOvVaw2DsbyTqLdrwPnPtFvm8CFH) that come with every IRL G1 race, which give 150 jewels for completing. Big events like New Years, Golden Week, and anniversaries also reward many gems and give free pulls. On average, it’s about 10-15k jewels a month total.

Friend Points
-------------

Friend points are acquired when people use the Support Card you set in your profile during a run. You also get quite a bit of them from PvP, 5000 from remaining in Class 6 or 4000 from remaining in Class 5.

People won’t typically use Support Cards that are below max limit break. It’s difficult to gain Friend Points through people using your card without an MLB SSR.

The Friend Point Shop sometimes has Pieces added to it, which you use to increase the number of stars an uma has. It typically costs around 20,000 points to purchase the full stack of 50 Pieces. You’ll mainly use them for Hint Books after getting Urara, though.

Trainer Medals
--------------

As of 2024/8/24, all circle points were converted into trainer medals.

Medals can be acquired through a number of ways.

*   Rental rewards with 10 each time someone uses the uma you set in your profile as a parent during their run, up to 100 per day
*   Monthly Circle ranking, fulfilling Circle requests for shoes or banners, which is 5 points per item, up to 100 per day, and the daily missions for checking your Circle and sending an item request
*   Stadium races, weekly rewards, and first time class promotions
*   Daily Races, up to 30 per day
*   Daily Legend Race, 20 per race
*   Completion of a training run, 200 each
*   Training run race rewards, 10 medals per race up to 100 per run

People won’t typically borrow an uma with less than 9\* in blue factors. Stamina and Power factors tend to be more desirable than Speed. Having a high amount of シナリオ factors also adds to your uma’s appeal, as well as uncommon pink factors like Dirt or Short.
